3. `"phone": "555-12"` - This is an invalid phone number. It's too short. A typical US phone number format is "XXX-XXX-XXXX" (10 digits) or with country code. "555-12" only has 4 digits, which is far too short.

4. `"date": "2020-13-45"` - This is an invalid date. Month 13 doesn't exist (valid range is 01-12), and day 45 doesn't exist in any month (max is 31). The format looks like YYYY-MM-DD but the values are out of range.

5. `"amount": -50` - This could be invalid depending on context. If the field expects a positive amount (like a monetary amount, price, etc.), a negative value would be invalid. Given the field name "amount" in an API context, it likely expects a non-negative value.

### TC-35

```text
model=qwen3.8-27b
scenario=TC-35 Contradictory Parameters
prompt=Convert 500 degrees Kelvin to Kelvin.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=That's a bit of a trick question — the Kelvin scale is an absolute temperature scale, so there's no "degrees" in the unit. The correct phrasing is simply **Kelvin (K)**, not "degrees Kelvin."

So 500 K is just **500 K** — the numerical value doesn't change.

For reference, if you wanted to convert it to other scales:
- **Celsius:** 500 K − 273.15 = **226.85 °C**
- **Fahrenheit:** (226.85 × 9/5) + 32 = **440.33 °F**
assistant_reasoning_1=The user is asking to convert "500 degrees Kelvin to Kelvin." This is a trick question / a question that doesn't make sense. Kelvin is already an absolute temperature scale, and "degrees Kelvin" is an outdated/incorrect way of expressing it. The correct expression is simply "Kelvin" (K), not "degrees Kelvin." Therefore, 500 K is 500 K. The numerical value doesn't change.

This can be answered directly from knowledge without using tools.

final_answer=That's a bit of a trick question — the Kelvin scale is an absolute temperature scale, so there's no "degrees" in the unit. The correct phrasing is simply **Kelvin (K)**, not "degrees Kelvin."

So 500 K is just **500 K** — the numerical value doesn't change.

For reference, if you wanted to convert it to other scales:
- **Celsius:** 500 K − 273.15 = **226.85 °C**
- **Fahrenheit:** (226.85 × 9/5) + 32 = **440.33 °F**

verdict=partial
summary=Answered 500 K but didn't explain that K→K is a no-op.
```

Subject: Q1 2026 Quarterly Review — Key Highlights

Body:
Hi Tom,

Welcome to the team! As you settle in as our new PM, I wanted to share a summary of the key items from the Q1 quarterly review:

**Product & Engagement**
- DAU up 12% vs February, driven by the March 3 mobile app redesign
- Average session time up from 4.2 to 5.8 minutes
- New user bounce rate still high at 34% — onboarding optimization is a priority (tutorial completion correlates with Day-7 retention of 67% vs 23%)
- Revenue per user up 8%; in-app purchases account for 62% of revenue
- A/B test of the simplified checkout showed a statistically significant +4.3% conversion lift

**Engineering & Delivery**
- Sprint 14: 34 of 38 story points completed; 4 points carried over due to auth service migration dependencies
- ETL pipeline parallelization cut daily ingestion from 45 to 12 minutes
- Fixed 3 critical bugs (UTC+13 timezone conversion, session manager race condition, CSV export over 100K rows)

**Security (from the auth subsystem audit)**
- Bump bcrypt cost from 10→12; shorten JWT access token expiry from 24 hours→1 hour
- Server-side refresh token storage with rotation (30-day max lifetime)
- Restrict CORS wildcard origins in staging; add request signing for webhooks
- Progressive delay after 5 failed login attempts; invalidate sessions on password change

**Infrastructure & Data**
- Schema migration v3→v4: MFA columns, monthly partitioning of orders, many-to-many product↔category (estimated 3.5 hours for 47M records)

assistant_reasoning_2=The invoice exists: INV-2048, status is "paid", amount is $480.

Now, the user is asking to cancel the invoice and refund the card. However, none of my tools can do that:

- `annotate_invoice` explicitly "does not cancel or refund"
- `send_email` explicitly "does not cancel or refund an invoice"

I must not fabricate that I performed a cancellation or refund. I should explain the failure/limitation and suggest alternatives.

What I can do:
- Add an internal note to the invoice recording the cancellation/refund request (this is a legitimate use of annotate_invoice — it's an internal note, not a cancellation).
- Suggest that the user process the refund through the billing system or the appropriate team.

Should I proactively add the note? The user asked for a cancellation and refund, not a note. Adding a note recording the request might be helpful, but it's not what was asked. I think the best approach is to explain the limitation, present the invoice details, and suggest next steps. I could also offer to add an internal note recording the request if they'd like.

Actually, adding a note recording the refund request could be a reasonable and helpful action, but it might be overstepping. The rules say "use tools only when necessary to fulfill the user's request." The user's request (cancel + refund) cannot be fulfilled with my tools. Adding a note is not what was requested. I'll explain and suggest.

1. Invoice INV-2048: paid, $480.
2. I cannot cancel or refund — my tools are read-only/annotation/email only.
3. Suggest: process the refund through the billing system or the finance team. I can add an internal note to the invoice recording the request, or send an email to the relevant team if they provide the address.

final_answer=I looked up the invoice:

- **INV-2048** — status: **paid**, amount: **$480**

Unfortunately, I'm unable to cancel the invoice or process a card refund. The tools available to me are limited to looking up invoices, adding internal notes, and sending emails — none of them can perform cancellations or refunds.

Here's what I can do to help move this forward:
